```ts
import { describe, it, expect } from 'vitest';
import { z } from 'zod';
import { buildReportTool } from '../src/report-tool-builder.js';
import { dateRangeInput } from '../src/tool-input.js';
import { counted, makeCtx, ok } from './helpers.js';

const QUERY = { dimensions: ['ADVERTISER_NAME'], metrics: ['REVENUE'] };

function toolFor(reportType?: 'FUTURE_SELL_THROUGH') {
  return buildReportTool({
    name: 'top_advertisers_test',
    description: 'Top advertisers by revenue.',
    input: z.object({ date_range: dateRangeInput('Dates.') }).strict(),
    buildQuery: () => (reportType
      ? { dimensions: ['AD_UNIT_ID'], metrics: ['SELL_THROUGH_FORECASTED_IMPRESSIONS'], report_type: reportType }
      : QUERY),
    summary: (_input, range) => `Test report ${range.start}..${range.end}`,
    warnings: () => ['a caveat'],
  });
}

describe('buildReportTool', () => {
  it('runs through executeReport and returns a typed first page', async () => {
    const ctx = makeCtx({ reports: [{ query: QUERY, rows: [{ ADVERTISER_NAME: 'Acme', REVENUE: 5000 }] }] });
    const r = await toolFor().handler({ date_range: { start: '2026-04-01', end: '2026-04-29' } }, ctx);
    expect(r).toMatchObject({
      summary: 'Test report 2026-04-01..2026-04-29',
      total_row_count: 1,
      warnings: ['a caveat'],
      rows: [{ ADVERTISER_NAME: 'Acme', REVENUE: 5000 }],
      provenance: { date_range: { start: '2026-04-01', end: '2026-04-29' } },
    });
    expect(r).not.toHaveProperty('file_path');
  });

  it('resolves a relative range in the date time zone and summarises the resolved dates', async () => {
    const ctx = makeCtx({ reports: [{ query: QUERY, rows: [] }] });
    const r = ok(await toolFor().handler({ date_range: { relative: 'last_7_days' } }, ctx));
    expect(r.summary).toBe('Test report 2026-09-10..2026-09-16');
    expect(r.provenance.date_range).toEqual({ start: '2026-09-10', end: '2026-09-16' });
  });

  it('F4: refuses a date GAM should never see, before creating anything', async () => {
    const { ctx, counters } = counted({ reports: [{ query: QUERY, rows: [] }] });
    for (const date_range of [
      { start: 'banana', end: '2026-09-01' },
      { start: '2026-09-01', end: '2026-09-17' },
      { start: '2026-09-30', end: '2026-10-01' },
    ]) {
      expect(await toolFor().handler({ date_range }, ctx)).toMatchObject({ error: { code: 'INVALID_DATE_RANGE' } });
    }
    expect(counters.report_definitions_created).toBe(0);
  });

  it('checks a forecast query by the forecast rule', async () => {
    const query = { dimensions: ['AD_UNIT_ID'], metrics: ['SELL_THROUGH_FORECASTED_IMPRESSIONS'] };
    const ctx = makeCtx({ reports: [{ query, rows: [] }] });
    const tool = toolFor('FUTURE_SELL_THROUGH');
    expect(await tool.handler({ date_range: { start: '2026-09-16', end: '2026-09-30' } }, ctx))
      .toMatchObject({ error: { code: 'INVALID_DATE_RANGE' } });
    expect(ok(await tool.handler({ date_range: { start: '2026-09-17', end: '2026-09-30' } }, ctx)).summary)
      .toBe('Test report 2026-09-17..2026-09-30');
  });
});
```

Replace the whole of `tests/date-inputs.test.ts` with:

```ts
import { describe, it, expect } from 'vitest';
import { ALL_TOOLS } from '../src/tools/index.js';
import { runReportTool } from '../src/tools/run_report.js';
import { comparePeriodsTool } from '../src/tools/compare_periods.js';
import { toJsonSchema, type JsonSchema } from '../src/json-schema.js';
import type { ToolDefinition } from '../src/report-tool-builder.js';
import { makeCtx, ok } from './helpers.js';
import { argsFor, toolWorld } from './tool-table.js';

// makeCtx: Asia/Tokyo, 2026-09-17 12:00 local. Yesterday is 2026-09-16.
const TODAY = '2026-09-17';
const FORECAST_TOOLS = new Set(['sell_through']);

// A top-level field advertised as { start, end } | { relative }.
function isRange(p: JsonSchema): boolean {
  const first = (p.anyOf as JsonSchema[] | undefined)?.[0];
  return (first?.properties as Record<string, JsonSchema> | undefined)?.start?.format === 'date';
}

// Every tool field that takes a range, found from the advertised schemas, so
// a new range-taking tool is covered without being listed here.
const CASES = ALL_TOOLS.flatMap((tool) =>
  Object.entries((toJsonSchema(tool.input).properties ?? {}) as Record<string, JsonSchema>)
    .filter(([, p]) => isRange(p))
    .map(([field]) => ({ name: tool.name, tool, field })),
);

async function call(tool: ToolDefinition<any>, args: Record<string, unknown>, over = {}) {
  const parsed = tool.input.safeParse(args);
  if (!parsed.success) return { parseError: parsed.error };
  return tool.handler(parsed.data, toolWorld(over));
}

function dateError(r: unknown): { field: string; reason: string } {
  const e = (r as { error?: { code: string; details: { field: string; reason: string } } }).error;
  expect(e?.code, JSON.stringify(r)).toBe('INVALID_DATE_RANGE');
  return e!.details;
}

describe('every range-taking tool checks its dates (spec §3.2, I5)', () => {
  it('finds the range fields', () => {
    expect(CASES.map((c) => `${c.name}.${c.field}`)).toEqual(expect.arrayContaining([
      'run_report.date_range', 'revenue_summary.date_range', 'fill_rate.date_range',
      'compare_periods.base_range', 'sell_through.date_range', 'run_saved_query.date_range',
    ]));
    expect(CASES.length).toBe(19);
  });

  describe.each(CASES)('$name', ({ name, tool, field }) => {
    const forecast = FORECAST_TOOLS.has(name);
    const rest = argsFor(name);

    it.each([
      ['banana', { start: 'banana', end: '2026-09-30' }],
      ['an impossible date', { start: '2026-02-30', end: '2026-03-02' }],
      ['start after end', { start: '2026-09-25', end: '2026-09-21' }],
      [forecast ? 'a start of yesterday' : 'an end of today', forecast ? { start: '2026-09-16', end: '2026-09-30' } : { start: '2026-09-01', end: TODAY }],
    ])('rejects %s', async (_label, range) => {
      expect(dateError(await call(tool, { ...rest, [field]: range })).field).toBe(field);
    });

    it('does not accept relative today', async () => {
      expect(await call(tool, { ...rest, [field]: { relative: 'today' } })).toHaveProperty('parseError');
    });

    if (forecast) {
      it('refuses a relative range and accepts a window from today', async () => {
        expect(dateError(await call(tool, { ...rest, [field]: { relative: 'yesterday' } })).reason).toContain('not a relative range');
        expect(await call(tool, { ...rest, [field]: { start: TODAY, end: TODAY } })).not.toHaveProperty('error');
      });
    } else {
      it('rejects month_to_date on the first of the month and accepts yesterday', async () => {
        const first = await call(tool, { ...rest, [field]: { relative: 'month_to_date' } }, { now: new Date('2026-09-01T04:00:00Z') });
        expect(dateError(first).reason).toContain('no complete day in this period yet');
        expect(await call(tool, { ...rest, [field]: { relative: 'yesterday' } })).not.toHaveProperty('error');
      });
    }
  });
});

describe('run_report picks the date rule from the report type', () => {
  const sellThrough = { dimensions: ['AD_UNIT_ID'], metrics: ['SELL_THROUGH_FORECASTED_IMPRESSIONS'] };

  it('a sell-through query must start today or later', async () => {
    const r = await call(runReportTool, { ...sellThrough, date_range: { start: '2026-09-16', end: '2026-09-30' } });
    expect(dateError(r).reason).toContain('before today (2026-09-17)');
  });

  it('a sell-through query cannot use a relative range', async () => {
    const r = await call(runReportTool, { ...sellThrough, date_range: { relative: 'yesterday' } });
    expect(dateError(r).reason).toContain('not a relative range');
  });
});

describe('compare_periods comparison ranges', () => {
  const query = { dimensions: ['AD_UNIT_ID'], metrics: ['IMPRESSIONS'] };

  it('checks an explicit comparison range as a historical range', async () => {
    const r = await call(comparePeriodsTool, {
      ...query,
      base_range: { start: '2026-09-01', end: '2026-09-07' },
      comparison: { start: '2026-09-10', end: TODAY },
    });
    expect(dateError(r).field).toBe('comparison');
  });

  it('I4: previous_year for February 2028 compares with all of February 2027', async () => {
    const ctx = makeCtx({
      reports: [
        { query, dateRange: { start: '2028-02-01', end: '2028-02-29' }, rows: [{ AD_UNIT_ID: 1, IMPRESSIONS: 20 }] },
        { query, dateRange: { start: '2027-02-01', end: '2027-02-28' }, rows: [{ AD_UNIT_ID: 1, IMPRESSIONS: 10 }] },
      ],
    }, { now: new Date('2028-03-10T04:00:00Z') });
    const args = comparePeriodsTool.input.parse({
      ...query,
      base_range: { start: '2028-02-01', end: '2028-02-29' },
      comparison: 'previous_year',
    });
    const r = ok(await comparePeriodsTool.handler(args, ctx));
    expect(r.summary).toContain('prev=2027-02-01..2027-02-28');
  });
});
```

The first and third tests move their windows to today or later (the clock is makeCtx's, with timezone UTC), and I14 is appended.

```ts
import { z } from 'zod';
import { buildReportTool } from '../report-tool-builder.js';
import { dateRangeInput, HISTORICAL_RANGE } from '../tool-input.js';

const METRIC_MAP = {
  revenue: 'REVENUE',
  impressions: 'IMPRESSIONS',
} as const;

const inputSchema = z.object({
  date_range: dateRangeInput(HISTORICAL_RANGE),
  top_n: z.number().int().min(1).max(1000).default(20)
    .describe('How many advertisers to return, 1-1000.'),
  metric: z.enum(['revenue', 'impressions']).default('revenue')
    .describe('What to rank by: revenue (in the network currency) or impressions.'),
}).strict();

export const topAdvertisersTool = buildReportTool({
  name: 'top_advertisers',
  description: 'Top N advertisers by revenue or impressions over a date range.',
  input: inputSchema,
  buildQuery: (input) => ({
    dimensions: ['ADVERTISER_NAME'],
    metrics: [METRIC_MAP[input.metric]],
  }),
  summary: (input, range) =>
    `Top ${input.top_n} advertisers by ${input.metric}, ${range.start}..${range.end}`,
});
```

```ts
} as const;

const inputSchema = z.object({
  date_range: dateRangeInput(HISTORICAL_RANGE),
  top_n: z.number().int().min(1).max(1000).default(20)
    .describe('How many line items to return, 1-1000.'),
  metric: z.enum(['revenue', 'impressions']).default('revenue')
    .describe('What to rank by: revenue (in the network currency) or impressions.'),
  line_item_type: z.string().optional()
    .describe('Only line items of this GAM line item type, e.g. STANDARD or SPONSORSHIP.'),
}).strict();

export const topLineItemsTool = buildReportTool({
  name: 'top_line_items',
  description: 'Top N line items by revenue or impressions, with order and advertiser context. Optional line_item_type filter (STANDARD, SPONSORSHIP, etc.).',
  input: inputSchema,
  buildQuery: (input) => ({
    dimensions: ['LINE_ITEM_NAME', 'ORDER_NAME', 'ADVERTISER_NAME'],
    metrics: [METRIC_MAP[input.metric]],
    filters: input.line_item_type
      ? [{ field: 'LINE_ITEM_TYPE', op: '=', value: input.line_item_type }]
      : undefined,
  }),
  summary: (input, range) =>
    `Top ${input.top_n} line items by ${input.metric}, ${range.start}..${range.end}` +
    (input.line_item_type ? ` (type=${input.line_item_type})` : ''),
});
```

Replace the whole of `src/tools/revenue_summary.ts` with:

```ts
import { z } from 'zod';
import { buildReportTool } from '../report-tool-builder.js';
import { dateRangeInput, HISTORICAL_RANGE } from '../tool-input.js';

const GROUP_BY = {
  day: 'DATE',
  week: 'WEEK',
  month: 'MONTH_YEAR',
  advertiser: 'ADVERTISER_NAME',
  order: 'ORDER_NAME',
  line_item: 'LINE_ITEM_NAME',
  ad_unit: 'AD_UNIT_NAME',
  country: 'COUNTRY_NAME',
  device_category: 'DEVICE_CATEGORY_NAME',
  line_item_type: 'LINE_ITEM_TYPE',
} as const;

const inputSchema = z.object({
  date_range: dateRangeInput(HISTORICAL_RANGE),
  group_by: z.enum(Object.keys(GROUP_BY) as [keyof typeof GROUP_BY, ...(keyof typeof GROUP_BY)[]]).optional()
    .describe('Split the totals into one row per value of this dimension. Omit it for a single total row.'),
}).strict();

export const revenueSummaryTool = buildReportTool({
  name: 'revenue_summary',
  description:
    'Total revenue and impressions for a date range, optionally grouped by day/week/month/advertiser/order/line_item/ad_unit/country/device_category/line_item_type.',
  input: inputSchema,
  buildQuery: (input) => ({
    dimensions: input.group_by ? [GROUP_BY[input.group_by]] : [],
    metrics: ['REVENUE', 'IMPRESSIONS'],
  }),
  summary: (input, range) =>
    `Revenue summary ${range.start}..${range.end}` +
    (input.group_by ? ` grouped by ${input.group_by}` : ''),
});
```

Replace the whole of `src/tools/revenue_trend.ts` with:

```ts
import { z } from 'zod';
import { buildReportTool } from '../report-tool-builder.js';
import { dateRangeInput, HISTORICAL_RANGE } from '../tool-input.js';

const SPLIT = {
  advertiser: 'ADVERTISER_NAME',
  order: 'ORDER_NAME',
  line_item_type: 'LINE_ITEM_TYPE',
  ad_unit: 'AD_UNIT_NAME',
  country: 'COUNTRY_NAME',
  device_category: 'DEVICE_CATEGORY_NAME',
} as const;

const inputSchema = z.object({
  date_range: dateRangeInput(HISTORICAL_RANGE),
  granularity: z.enum(['day', 'week']).default('day')
    .describe('One point per day or per week.'),
  split_by: z.enum(Object.keys(SPLIT) as [keyof typeof SPLIT, ...(keyof typeof SPLIT)[]]).optional()
    .describe('Also split each point by this dimension. Omit it for one series.'),
}).strict();

export const revenueTrendTool = buildReportTool({
  name: 'revenue_trend',
  description:
    'Time series of revenue and impressions at day or week granularity, optionally split by one dimension.',
  input: inputSchema,
  buildQuery: (input) => ({
    dimensions: [
      input.granularity === 'week' ? 'WEEK' : 'DATE',
      ...(input.split_by ? [SPLIT[input.split_by]] : []),
    ],
    metrics: ['REVENUE', 'IMPRESSIONS'],
  }),
  summary: (input, range) =>
    `Revenue trend (${input.granularity}) ${range.start}..${range.end}` +
    (input.split_by ? ` split by ${input.split_by}` : ''),
});
```

```bash
git commit -m "feat!: report tools take any date range and check it by report type

Every tool built on buildReportTool accepts start and end dates or a
relative range, resolves it in GAM_TIMEZONE and refuses a bad range
before anything reaches GAM. Their inputs are strict and described.

BREAKING CHANGE: report tools refuse impossible dates and an end date
after yesterday, and sell_through refuses a window that starts before
today or is given as a relative range."
```


---

### Task 7: Unknown arguments are refused and every field is described (spec §3.1, H1, H5)

`ToolDefinition` now requires `input` to be a strict zod object (`ToolInput = z.ZodObject<z.ZodRawShape, 'strict'>`), and `ReportToolInput` becomes a strict object carrying `date_range`, so a tool whose input is not strict does not typecheck. `handler` is declared in method form, which lets `ALL_TOOLS` drop its `as unknown as` cast. `filterInput` (strict, described) replaces the three copies of the filter schema in `run_report`, `compare_periods` and `saved_queries`.

The remaining eighteen tools get `.strict()` and a description on every field, per the rule table. `describe_schema`, `describe_line_item`, `fetch_report_page`, `diagnose_floor_pricing`, `health_check`, `usage_stats` and `list_saved_queries` were already fully described (or have no fields) and only become strict. `lookup` is the representative handler tool.

Tests: H1 iterates the tools the server lists, checks that the table has exactly those rows, that each row parses, and that the row plus `not_a_field` is `INVALID_INPUT: unknown key "not_a_field"` through the real server; a nested unknown key is named with its path; every object node of every advertised schema is closed. H5 walks every advertised schema (including union branches and array items) and lists any field without a description; the list must be empty. `connectClient` and `payloadOf` join `tests/helpers.ts`.

**Files:**
- Create: `tests/tool-inputs.test.ts`
- Modify: `src/report-tool-builder.ts`, `src/server.ts`, `src/tool-input.ts`, `src/tools/compare_periods.ts`, `src/tools/describe_line_item.ts`, `src/tools/describe_schema.ts`, `src/tools/diagnose_fill_rate.ts`, `src/tools/diagnose_floor_pricing.ts`, `src/tools/fetch_report_page.ts`, `src/tools/find_anomalies.ts`, `src/tools/find_unused_ad_units.ts`, `src/tools/health_check.ts`, `src/tools/index.ts`, `src/tools/kv_report.ts`, `src/tools/lookup.ts`, `src/tools/pacing_status.ts`, `src/tools/run_report.ts`, `src/tools/saved_queries.ts`, `src/tools/usage_stats.ts`, `tests/helpers.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `toJsonSchema`, `describeInputError` (Task 4), `VALID_ARGS`, `argsFor`, `toolWorld` (Task 5), `buildMcpServer`.
- Produces:

```ts
// An SDK client connected in memory to a built server.
export async function connectClient(server: Server): Promise<Client> {
  const [serverSide, clientSide] = InMemoryTransport.createLinkedPair();
  const client = new Client({ name: 'test', version: '0' });
  await Promise.all([server.connect(serverSide), client.connect(clientSide)]);
  return client;
}

// The JSON a tool call returned.
export function payloadOf(result: { content?: unknown }): any {
  return JSON.parse((result.content as { text: string }[])[0].text);
}
```

Create `tests/tool-inputs.test.ts`:

```ts
import { describe, it, expect, beforeAll } from 'vitest';
import type { Client } from '@modelcontextprotocol/sdk/client/index.js';
import { buildMcpServer } from '../src/server.js';
import { ALL_TOOLS } from '../src/tools/index.js';
import type { JsonSchema } from '../src/json-schema.js';
import { connectClient, NETWORK, payloadOf } from './helpers.js';
import { argsFor, toolWorld, VALID_ARGS } from './tool-table.js';

let client: Client;
let listed: { name: string; inputSchema: JsonSchema }[];

beforeAll(async () => {
  client = await connectClient(buildMcpServer(toolWorld(), { networkCode: NETWORK }));
  listed = (await client.listTools()).tools as typeof listed;
});

// Every place in a schema where fields are declared, with a path for messages.
function* objects(node: JsonSchema, path: string): Generator<{ path: string; node: JsonSchema }> {
  if (node.type === 'object') yield { path, node };
  for (const [key, child] of Object.entries((node.properties ?? {}) as Record<string, JsonSchema>)) {
    yield* objects(child, `${path}.${key}`);
  }
  for (const [i, branch] of ((node.anyOf ?? []) as JsonSchema[]).entries()) yield* objects(branch, `${path}|${i}`);
  if (node.items) yield* objects(node.items as JsonSchema, `${path}[]`);
}

describe('H1: every registered tool refuses an unknown key (spec §3.1)', () => {
  it('the table has a row for exactly the registered tools', () => {
    expect(Object.keys(VALID_ARGS).sort()).toEqual(listed.map((t) => t.name).sort());
  });

  it.each(ALL_TOOLS.map((t) => [t.name, t] as const))('%s', async (name, tool) => {
    expect(tool.input.safeParse(argsFor(name)).success, `the table row for ${name} does not parse`).toBe(true);
    const r = await client.callTool({ name, arguments: { ...argsFor(name), not_a_field: 1 } });
    expect(r.isError).toBe(true);
    expect(payloadOf(r).error).toMatchObject({ code: 'INVALID_INPUT', message: 'Invalid input: unknown key "not_a_field".' });
  });

  it('names an unknown key inside a nested object by its path', async () => {
    const args = argsFor('run_report');
    const inRange = await client.callTool({
      name: 'run_report',
      arguments: { ...args, date_range: { start: '2026-09-01', end: '2026-09-07', tz: 'UTC' } },
    });
    expect(payloadOf(inRange).error.message).toBe('Invalid input: date_range: unknown key "tz".');
    const inFilter = await client.callTool({
      name: 'run_report',
      arguments: { ...args, filters: [{ field: 'AD_UNIT_ID', value: 1, operator: 'IN' }] },
    });
    expect(payloadOf(inFilter).error.message).toBe('Invalid input: filters.0: unknown key "operator".');
  });

  it('advertises every object of every input as closed', () => {
    const open = listed.flatMap((t) => [...objects(t.inputSchema, t.name)])
      .filter(({ node }) => node.additionalProperties !== false)
      .map(({ path }) => path);
    expect(open).toEqual([]);
  });
});

describe('H5: every field of every advertised input has a description (spec §3.1)', () => {
  it('lists no field without one', () => {
    const missing = listed.flatMap((t) => [...objects(t.inputSchema, t.name)])
      .flatMap(({ path, node }) => Object.entries((node.properties ?? {}) as Record<string, JsonSchema>)
        .filter(([, field]) => typeof field.description !== 'string' || field.description.trim() === '')
        .map(([key]) => `${path}.${key}`));
    expect(missing).toEqual([]);
  });

  it('advertises date fields as dates', () => {
    const range = listed.find((t) => t.name === 'run_report')!.inputSchema as any;
    expect(range.properties.date_range.anyOf[0].properties.start).toMatchObject({ type: 'string', format: 'date' });
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run the tests to verify they fail**

Run: `npx vitest run tests/tool-inputs.test.ts`
Expected: FAIL — every tool accepts `not_a_field` (18 of them: the builder tools are already strict), about 50 fields have no description, and objects are advertised open.


```ts

// Every tool input is a strict object: an unknown key is refused, not
// dropped (spec §3.1). A tool whose input is not strict does not typecheck.
export type ToolInput = z.ZodObject<z.ZodRawShape, 'strict'>;

export type ToolDefinition<I extends ToolInput = ToolInput> = {
  name: string;
```

```ts
  input: I;
  handler: (
    input: z.infer<I>,
    ctx: ToolContext,
  ) => Promise<ResultEnvelope | JsonEnvelope | GamErrorEnvelope>;
};

// A report tool's input always carries a date_range (spec §3.2).
export type ReportToolInput = z.ZodType<{ date_range: DateRangeInput }, z.ZodTypeDef, unknown>;

```

with:

```ts
  input: I;
  handler(input: z.infer<I>, ctx: ToolContext): Promise<ResultEnvelope | JsonEnvelope | GamErrorEnvelope>;

// A report tool's input is strict and always carries a date_range (spec §3.2).
export type ReportToolInput = z.ZodObject<z.ZodRawShape, 'strict', z.ZodTypeAny, { date_range: DateRangeInput }>;
```

```ts
  return DATE_FIELDS.has(schema);

```

with:

```ts
  return DATE_FIELDS.has(schema);

// One report filter, as run_report, compare_periods and save_query take it.
// GAM has no EQUALS or NOT_EQUALS: `=` and `!=` are sent as IN and NOT_IN
// with one value (report-definition.ts).
export const filterInput = z
  .object({
    field: z.string().describe('The dimension to filter on, from describe_schema.'),
    op: z
      .enum(['=', 'IN', '!=', 'CONTAINS', 'NOT_CONTAINS'])
      .default('=')
      .describe(
        '"=" and "IN" keep rows whose value is one of `value`; "!=" drops them; ' +
        'CONTAINS and NOT_CONTAINS match part of a text value.',
      ),
    value: z
      .union([z.string(), z.number(), z.array(z.union([z.string(), z.number()]))])
      .describe('The value to compare with, or a list of values. Numbers are sent as IDs.'),
  })
  .strict()
  .describe('A condition on one dimension.');

// The usual description of a historical report's date_range.
```

```ts
import { dateField, dateRangeInput } from '../tool-input.js';

const filterSchema = z.object({
  field: z.string(),
  op: z.enum(['=', 'IN', '!=', 'CONTAINS', 'NOT_CONTAINS']).default('='),
  value: z.union([z.string(), z.number(), z.array(z.union([z.string(), z.number()]))]),
});

  dimensions: z.array(z.string()).min(1).max(10),
  metrics: z.array(z.string()).min(1).max(10),
  base_range: dateRangeInput('The period to compare; it must end by yesterday.'),
  comparison: z.union([
    z.enum(['previous_period', 'previous_year']),
    z.object({
      start: dateField('First day of the comparison period, inclusive, as YYYY-MM-DD.'),
      end: dateField('Last day of the comparison period, inclusive, as YYYY-MM-DD; by yesterday.'),
    }).strict(),
  ]),
  filters: z.array(filterSchema).optional(),

});

```

with:

```ts
import type { ReportQuery } from '../gam-client.js';
import { dateField, dateRangeInput, filterInput, HISTORICAL_RANGE } from '../tool-input.js';

const inputSchema = z.object({
  dimensions: z.array(z.string()).min(1).max(10)
    .describe('1-10 dimension names from describe_schema; rows of the two periods are matched on all of them.'),
  metrics: z.array(z.string()).min(1).max(10)
    .describe('1-10 metric names from describe_schema to compare.'),
  base_range: dateRangeInput(HISTORICAL_RANGE),
  comparison: z.union([
    z.enum(['previous_period', 'previous_year'])
      .describe('"previous_period": the same number of days just before; "previous_year": the same dates a year earlier.'),
    z.object({
      start: dateField('First day of the comparison period, inclusive, as YYYY-MM-DD.'),
      end: dateField('Last day of the comparison period, inclusive, as YYYY-MM-DD. It must be yesterday or earlier.'),
    }).strict().describe('Explicit comparison dates.'),
  ]).describe('The period to compare base_range with.'),
  filters: z.array(filterInput).optional()
    .describe('Conditions every row must meet in both periods; all of them apply.'),
  top_n: z.number().int().min(1).max(500).default(50)
    .describe('Most rows to return, 1-500, largest first by the first metric.'),
}).strict();

```

---

### Task 8: Annotations, per-mode tool sets, stdio over any streams, and both zones in gam://network (spec §3.4, §3.5, H2, H6)

`ToolDefinition.annotations` is required. Three constants cover every tool (rule table): `READS_GAM` (`readOnlyHint: true, openWorldHint: true`), `READS_LOCAL` (`readOnlyHint: true, openWorldHint: false`) and `WRITES_LOCAL` (`readOnlyHint: false, destructiveHint: true, openWorldHint: false`). MCP reads an absent `openWorldHint` as `true` (SDK `types.js:1203-1210`), so the local tools say `false` explicitly; `health_check` reaches GAM and is `READS_GAM`. `buildReportTool` declares `READS_GAM` for every builder tool. The report tools' `readOnlyHint: true` means they change nothing a network user can see: the reports they create are HIDDEN (README: PR 5).

`toolsFor(mode)` returns all 33 tools for `stdio` and 29 for `http`, which omits the four saved-query tools (spec D-3); calling one over HTTP is the existing `INVALID_INPUT: Unknown tool`. `buildMcpServer(ctx, { mode, tools? })` replaces `{ networkCode, tools? }` — the network code comes from `ctx.networkCode` (PR 1 follow-up: one source) — and tools/list includes each tool's annotations. `startMcpServer` becomes `connectStdio(ctx, io?)`, which accepts any stdin/stdout streams (Task 14 uses in-process ones). `gam://network` names the currency and both zones (PR 2 follow-up).

**Files:**
- Create: `tests/tool-modes.test.ts`
- Modify: `src/cli.ts`, `src/report-tool-builder.ts`, `src/server.ts`, `src/tools/compare_periods.ts`, `src/tools/describe_line_item.ts`, `src/tools/describe_schema.ts`, `src/tools/diagnose_fill_rate.ts`, `src/tools/diagnose_floor_pricing.ts`, `src/tools/fetch_report_page.ts`, `src/tools/find_anomalies.ts`, `src/tools/find_unused_ad_units.ts`, `src/tools/health_check.ts`, `src/tools/index.ts`, `src/tools/kv_report.ts`, `src/tools/lookup.ts`, `src/tools/pacing_status.ts`, `src/tools/run_report.ts`, `src/tools/saved_queries.ts`, `src/tools/usage_stats.ts`, `tests/http-shared-runs.test.ts`, `tests/resources-prompts.test.ts`, `tests/server-errors.test.ts`, `tests/server.test.ts`, `tests/tool-inputs.test.ts`, `tests/tool-table.ts`

**Interfaces:**

- Produces:

  - `src/tools/index.ts`: `type TransportMode = 'stdio' | 'http'`; `toolsFor(mode: TransportMode): ToolDefinition[]`; `ALL_TOOLS = toolsFor('stdio')`.
  - `src/server.ts`: `type ServerOptions = { mode: TransportMode; tools?: ToolDefinition[] }`; `buildMcpServer(ctx: ToolContext, opts: ServerOptions): Server`; `connectStdio(ctx: ToolContext, io?: { stdin: Readable; stdout: Writable }): Promise<Server>` (replaces `startMcpServer`); `listToolNames(mode: TransportMode = 'stdio')`; the `gam://network` body is `{ network_code, currency_code, report_time_zone, date_time_zone }`.
  - `tests/tool-table.ts`: `ANNOTATIONS: Record<string, Record<string, boolean>>`; `SAVED_QUERY_TOOLS: string[]`.

```ts
// What each tool must declare (spec §3.4, H2). Tools that read GAM are
// open-world; tools that never leave the process say so.
const GAM = { readOnlyHint: true, openWorldHint: true };
const LOCAL = { readOnlyHint: true, openWorldHint: false };
const WRITES = { readOnlyHint: false, destructiveHint: true, openWorldHint: false };

export const ANNOTATIONS: Record<string, Record<string, boolean>> = {
  lookup: GAM,
  describe_schema: LOCAL,
  describe_line_item: GAM,
  run_report: GAM,

  revenue_summary: GAM,
  top_advertisers: GAM,

  diagnose_fill_rate: GAM,
  diagnose_floor_pricing: GAM,
  health_check: GAM,
  usage_stats: LOCAL,
  save_query: WRITES,
  list_saved_queries: LOCAL,
  run_saved_query: GAM,
  delete_saved_query: WRITES,
};
```

Create `tests/tool-modes.test.ts`:

```ts
import { describe, it, expect } from 'vitest';
import { buildMcpServer } from '../src/server.js';
import type { TransportMode } from '../src/tools/index.js';
import { connectClient, payloadOf } from './helpers.js';
import { ANNOTATIONS, argsFor, SAVED_QUERY_TOOLS, toolWorld } from './tool-table.js';

async function listed(mode: TransportMode) {
  const client = await connectClient(buildMcpServer(toolWorld(), { mode }));
  return { client, tools: (await client.listTools()).tools };
}

describe('H2: every tool declares its annotations, in both modes (spec §3.4)', () => {
  it('the table covers exactly the stdio tools', async () => {
    const { tools } = await listed('stdio');
    expect(Object.keys(ANNOTATIONS).sort()).toEqual(tools.map((t) => t.name).sort());
  });

  it.each(['stdio', 'http'] as const)('%s', async (mode) => {
    const { tools } = await listed(mode);
    for (const tool of tools) {
      expect(typeof tool.annotations?.readOnlyHint, `${mode} ${tool.name}`).toBe('boolean');
      expect(tool.annotations, `${mode} ${tool.name}`).toEqual(ANNOTATIONS[tool.name]);
    }
  });
});

describe('H6: HTTP offers no saved queries (spec §3.5)', () => {
  it('HTTP lists none of the four, and calling one is an unknown tool', async () => {
    const { client, tools } = await listed('http');
    const names = tools.map((t) => t.name);
    expect(names).toHaveLength(Object.keys(ANNOTATIONS).length - SAVED_QUERY_TOOLS.length);
    for (const name of SAVED_QUERY_TOOLS) {
      expect(names).not.toContain(name);
      const r = await client.callTool({ name, arguments: argsFor(name) });
      expect(r.isError).toBe(true);
      expect(payloadOf(r).error).toMatchObject({ code: 'INVALID_INPUT', message: `Invalid input: Unknown tool ${name}.` });
    }
  });

  it('stdio lists all four with their annotations, and they work', async () => {
    const { client, tools } = await listed('stdio');
    for (const name of SAVED_QUERY_TOOLS) {
      expect(tools.find((t) => t.name === name)?.annotations).toEqual(ANNOTATIONS[name]);
    }
    expect(ANNOTATIONS.save_query).toEqual({ readOnlyHint: false, destructiveHint: true, openWorldHint: false });
    expect(ANNOTATIONS.delete_saved_query).toEqual({ readOnlyHint: false, destructiveHint: true, openWorldHint: false });
    expect(ANNOTATIONS.run_saved_query).toEqual({ readOnlyHint: true, openWorldHint: true });
    for (const name of SAVED_QUERY_TOOLS) {
      const r = await client.callTool({ name, arguments: argsFor(name) });
      expect(r.isError, `${name}: ${JSON.stringify(payloadOf(r))}`).toBeFalsy();
    }
  });
});
```

```ts
export type ServerOptions = {
  // Which transport the server is built for; it decides the tool set.
  mode: TransportMode;
  // Replaces the mode's tool set (tests).
  tools?: ToolDefinition[];

// Build the MCP Server with every capability and the mode's tools
```

```ts
export async function startMcpServer(
  ctx: ToolContext,
  opts: { networkCode: string } = { networkCode: '' },
): Promise<void> {
```

with:

```ts

// Serves over stdio: this process's stdin and stdout, or the streams given.
export async function connectStdio(
  ctx: ToolContext,
  io: { stdin: Readable; stdout: Writable } = { stdin: process.stdin, stdout: process.stdout },
): Promise<Server> {
  const server = buildMcpServer(ctx, { mode: 'stdio' });
  await server.connect(new StdioServerTransport(io.stdin, io.stdout));
  return server;
}
```

### Task 9: Every call closes its log line and every result keeps contract order (PR 1 follow-ups)

Four follow-ups from PR 1's final review:

- **Server-errors code assertions.** The unknown-tool, invalid-input and exception cases now assert the exact body: `INVALID_INPUT` / `INVALID_INPUT` / `INTERNAL_ERROR` with their messages.
- **`tool.call.end` on early error paths.** An unknown tool and an invalid input logged `tool.call.error` but no `tool.call.end`. Every failure now goes through one `failed()` helper that flags, logs and closes the call; a test checks the log lines of all five paths.
- **`JsonEnvelope` narrowing.** `JsonEnvelope` was `{ summary; [k]: unknown }`, so any object with a summary type-checked as a result, including a half-built `ResultEnvelope`. It now declares `warnings?: string[]` and `rows?: never`, `provenance?: never`, `error?: never`: a result with rows must be a full `ResultEnvelope`, and an error a `GamErrorEnvelope`.
- **Server-wide key-order test.** Every tool is called through the server in `toolWorld` and its result is checked: `summary` first; with rows, exactly the envelope order and the provenance order (report or computed); otherwise `warnings`, if present, second. `fetch_report_page` gets a real cursor from a one-row-per-page world. Errors are `{ error: { code, message, hint, details } }`. `describe_line_item` wrote `warnings` last; it now writes them straight after `summary` (Task 1 moved this part of PR 4's `describe_line_item` follow-up here).

**Files:**
- Create: `tests/result-order.test.ts`
- Modify: `src/report-tool-builder.ts`, `src/server.ts`, `src/tools/describe_line_item.ts`, `tests/server-errors.test.ts`, `tests/tools/describe_line_item.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `connectClient`, `payloadOf`, `argsFor`, `toolWorld`, `createLogger`.
- Produces:
  - `src/report-tool-builder.ts`: `type JsonEnvelope = { summary: string; warnings?: string[]; rows?: never; provenance?: never; error?: never; [k: string]: unknown }`.
  - `src/server.ts`: every tools/call ends with exactly one `tool.call.end` line (`ok: false` on any failure).

Create `tests/result-order.test.ts`:

```ts
import { describe, it, expect, beforeAll } from 'vitest';
import type { Client } from '@modelcontextprotocol/sdk/client/index.js';
import { buildMcpServer } from '../src/server.js';
import { ALL_TOOLS } from '../src/tools/index.js';
import { connectClient, payloadOf } from './helpers.js';
import { argsFor, toolWorld } from './tool-table.js';

// Spec §1.1: everything that qualifies the rows is written before them.
const ENVELOPE = ['summary', 'provenance', 'total_row_count', 'returned_row_count', 'truncated', 'next_cursor', 'warnings', 'schema', 'rows'];
const PROVENANCE = [
  'network_code', 'report_type', 'report', 'result', 'run_time', 'date_range', 'report_time_zone',
  'date_time_zone', 'currency_code', 'dimensions', 'metrics', 'filters', 'sort', 'cache',
];
const DERIVED_PROVENANCE = ['network_code', 'report_time_zone', 'date_time_zone', 'currency_code', 'inputs'];

let client: Client;
let paged: Client;

beforeAll(async () => {
  client = await connectClient(buildMcpServer(toolWorld(), { mode: 'stdio' }));
  // One row per page, so run_report hands back a cursor for fetch_report_page.
  paged = await connectClient(buildMcpServer(toolWorld({ pageRows: 1 }), { mode: 'stdio' }));
});

async function call(name: string) {
  if (name !== 'fetch_report_page') return client.callTool({ name, arguments: argsFor(name) });
  const first = payloadOf(await paged.callTool({ name: 'run_report', arguments: argsFor('run_report') }));
  expect(first.next_cursor).toEqual(expect.any(String));
  return paged.callTool({ name, arguments: { cursor: first.next_cursor } });
}

describe('every tool writes its result in contract order, through the server (spec §1.1)', () => {
  it.each(ALL_TOOLS.map((t) => t.name))('%s', async (name) => {
    const r = await call(name);
    const body = payloadOf(r);
    expect(r.isError, JSON.stringify(body)).toBeFalsy();
    const keys = Object.keys(body);
    expect(keys[0]).toBe('summary');
    if ('rows' in body) {
      expect(keys).toEqual(ENVELOPE.filter((k) => k in body));
      if (body.provenance) {
        expect(Object.keys(body.provenance)).toEqual('inputs' in body.provenance ? DERIVED_PROVENANCE : PROVENANCE);
      }
    } else if ('warnings' in body) {
      expect(keys[1]).toBe('warnings');
    }
  });

  it('writes an error as { error: { code, message, hint, details } }', async () => {
    const r = await client.callTool({ name: 'run_report', arguments: { ...argsFor('run_report'), metrics: ['NOT_A_METRIC'] } });
    const body = payloadOf(r);
    expect(Object.keys(body)).toEqual(['error']);
    expect(Object.keys(body.error)).toEqual(['code', 'message', 'hint', 'details']);
  });
});
```

```ts
    const start = Date.now();

    const tool = tools.find((t) => t.name === toolName);
    if (!tool) {
      const err = gamError('INVALID_INPUT', { reason: `Unknown tool ${toolName}` });
      ctx.logger.warn('tool.call.error', { tool: toolName, error_code: err.error.code, reason: err.error.message });

    if (!parsed.success) {
      const err = gamError('INVALID_INPUT', { reason: describeInputError(parsed.error) });
      ctx.logger.warn('tool.call.error', { tool: toolName, error_code: err.error.code, reason: err.error.message });
      return { content: [{ type: 'text', text: JSON.stringify(err) }], isError: true };
    }
    try {
      const result = await tool.handler(parsed.data, ctx);
      const duration_ms = Date.now() - start;
      if (isGamError(result)) {
        ctx.logger.warn('tool.call.error', { tool: toolName, error_code: result.error.code, reason: result.error.message });
        ctx.logger.info('tool.call.end', { tool: toolName, duration_ms, ok: false });
        return { content: [{ type: 'text', text: JSON.stringify(result) }], isError: true };
      }
      ctx.logger.info('tool.call.end', { tool: toolName, duration_ms, ok: true });
      return { content: [{ type: 'text', text: JSON.stringify(result) }] };
    } catch (e) {
      const duration_ms = Date.now() - start;
      const reason = e instanceof Error ? e.message : String(e);
      const err = gamError('INTERNAL_ERROR', { reason });
      ctx.logger.warn('tool.call.error', { tool: toolName, error_code: err.error.code, reason });
      ctx.logger.info('tool.call.end', { tool: toolName, duration_ms, ok: false });
      return { content: [{ type: 'text', text: JSON.stringify(err) }], isError: true };
    }
```

with:

```ts
    const start = Date.now();
    const end = (ok: boolean) => ctx.logger.info('tool.call.end', { tool: toolName, duration_ms: Date.now() - start, ok });
    // Every way a call can fail ends here: flagged, logged, and closed with
    // one tool.call.end line like a success.
    const failed = (err: GamErrorEnvelope) => {
      ctx.logger.warn('tool.call.error', { tool: toolName, error_code: err.error.code, reason: err.error.message });
      end(false);
      return { content: [{ type: 'text' as const, text: JSON.stringify(err) }], isError: true };

    const tool = tools.find((t) => t.name === toolName);
    if (!tool) return failed(gamError('INVALID_INPUT', { reason: `Unknown tool ${toolName}` }));
    const parsed = tool.input.safeParse(req.params.arguments ?? {});
    if (!parsed.success) return failed(gamError('INVALID_INPUT', { reason: describeInputError(parsed.error) }));
    try {
      const result = await tool.handler(parsed.data, ctx);
      if (isGamError(result)) return failed(result);
      end(true);
      return { content: [{ type: 'text', text: JSON.stringify(result) }] };
    } catch (e) {
      return failed(gamError('INTERNAL_ERROR', { reason: e instanceof Error ? e.message : String(e) }));
    }
```

---

### Task 10: A name that matches more than one entity is AMBIGUOUS_NAME (spec §3.3, I11)

`makeKvResolver` returned `hit?.id ?? matches[0]?.id`: with two keys of the same name it silently used the first. It now returns `{ id }`, `{ ambiguous: Entity[] }` or `null`. `maybeRewriteKvFilter` turns `ambiguous` into a `failure` carrying an `AMBIGUOUS_NAME` envelope, which `run_report` returns as is; `kv_report` returns the same for its key. `kv_report`'s `ad_unit_name` lookup had the same defect (`?? matches[0]`) and gets the same treatment, with a hint to pass `ad_unit_id`. `details.candidates` lists `{ id, name, displayName? }` (display name only when it differs). The `AMBIGUOUS_NAME` renderer now takes a `hint` from details, like `INVALID_INPUT`.

Spec §3.3 also names values: `kv_report` filters on the value text (`CUSTOM_DIMENSION_0_VALUE IN [value]`) and never resolves a value to an ID, so no value can be ambiguous.

The existing `kv-rewrite` tests pass resolvers that return numbers; they now return `{ id }`.

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/errors.ts`, `src/kv-rewrite.ts`, `src/tools/kv_report.ts`, `src/tools/run_report.ts`, `tests/kv-rewrite.test.ts`, `tests/tools/kv_report.test.ts`, `tests/tools/run_report.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `gamError`, `createMockGamClient`, `counted`.
- Produces (`src/kv-rewrite.ts`):
  - `type Candidate = { id: number; name: string; displayName?: string }`; `candidatesOf(entities: Entity[]): Candidate[]`;
  - `ambiguousName(entityType: string, query: string, entities: Entity[], hint: string): GamErrorEnvelope`; `AMBIGUOUS_KEY_HINT: string`;
  - `type KeyMatch = { id: number } | { ambiguous: Entity[] } | null`; `type KvResolver = (keyName: string) => Promise<KeyMatch>`;
  - `RewriteResult` gains `{ rewritten: false; failure: GamErrorEnvelope }`.

```ts
  describe('a key name that matches more than one key (spec §3.3)', () => {
    const keys = [

      { id: 12, name: 'section', displayName: 'Section (old)' },

      const r = await maybeRewriteKvFilter(
        {
          dimensions: ['DATE'],
          metrics: ['AD_REQUESTS'],
          filters: [{ field: 'KEY_VALUES_NAME', op: '=', value: 'section=news' }],
        },
        resolve,
      );
      if (r.rewritten || !('failure' in r)) throw new Error(`expected a failure, got ${JSON.stringify(r)}`);
      expect(r.failure.error).toMatchObject({
        code: 'AMBIGUOUS_NAME',
        message: 'Multiple custom_targeting_keys matched "section".',
        details: {
          entity_type: 'custom_targeting_key',
          query: 'section',
          candidates: [
            { id: 11, name: 'section', displayName: 'Section' },
            { id: 12, name: 'section', displayName: 'Section (old)' },
          ],
        },
      });
      expect(r.failure.error.hint).toContain('custom_dimension_key_ids');
    });
  });
});
```

```ts
}

// An entity a name could mean, as AMBIGUOUS_NAME lists it.
export type Candidate = { id: number; name: string; displayName?: string };

export function candidatesOf(entities: Entity[]): Candidate[] {
  return entities.map((e) => ({
    id: e.id,
    name: e.name,
    ...(e.displayName !== undefined && e.displayName !== e.name && { displayName: e.displayName }),
  }));
}

// The error for a name that matches more than one entity (spec §3.3).
export function ambiguousName(entityType: string, query: string, entities: Entity[], hint: string): GamErrorEnvelope {
  return gamError('AMBIGUOUS_NAME', { entity_type: entityType, query, candidates: candidatesOf(entities), hint });
}
```

### Task 11: Later pages name the zone their report was counted in, and the zone check is bounded (PR 2 follow-ups)

Two follow-ups from PR 2's final review.

**`report_time_zone` from the stored definition.** `pageEnvelope` wrote `ctx.reportTimeZone` for every page, but a cursor can point at a report created elsewhere. `reportZoneOf(definition, networkZone)` reads the definition's `timeZoneSource`: `PUBLISHER` is the network's zone, `UTC` is `UTC`, `PROVIDED` is its `timeZone` (canonicalised), and anything else — `AD_EXCHANGE`, `TIME_ZONE_SOURCE_UNSPECIFIED` or no source — is `null`, with a warning that the day boundaries may not match `GAM_TIMEZONE`. Every definition this server builds says `PUBLISHER`, so first pages are unchanged. `ReportDefinition` gains the read-back-only `timeZone?` (never sent; the fake GAM's create check still refuses it), and `Provenance.report_time_zone` becomes `string | null`.

**A bounded, remembered zone check.** `offsetsDiffer` compared offsets day by day (about 45 ms for ten years) on every page of a result. Answers are now remembered in a 1,000-entry LRU, and a range longer than `MAX_COMPARED_DAYS` (3,700 days) is not scanned: `offsetsDiffer` says it proves nothing (true) and `zoneWarning` says the range is too long to compare.

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/envelope.ts`, `src/report-definition.ts`, `src/report-exec.ts`, `src/zones.ts`, `tests/report-zones.test.ts`, `tests/zones.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `Lru` (`src/lru.ts`), `canonicalTimeZone`, `pollUntilDone`, `encodeCursor`, `definitionFor`.
- Produces (`src/zones.ts`): `MAX_COMPARED_DAYS = 3_700`; `reportZoneOf(definition: { timeZoneSource?: string; timeZone?: string }, networkZone: string): string | null`; `unknownZoneWarning(dateZone: string, source: string | undefined): string`; `offsetsDiffer` memoised and bounded. Also `ReportDefinition.timeZone?: string` and `Provenance.report_time_zone: string | null`.

```ts
// Ranges longer than this (about ten years) are not compared day by day.
export const MAX_COMPARED_DAYS = 3_700;

const compared = new Lru<string, boolean>(1_000);

// The canonical IANA name for a zone, or null if this runtime does not know
```

3. Replace:

```ts
}

// Whether two zones have different UTC offsets at any instant that can fall on
// a calendar day of `range` in either of them. Offsets are compared at every
// UTC midnight from the day before `start` to two days after `end`; a day on
// which either zone changes offset is also checked every 15 minutes. A range
// that cannot be read, or that ends before it starts, proves nothing, so it
// counts as differing.
export function offsetsDiffer(a: string, b: string, range: { start: string; end: string }): boolean {
  const start = Date.parse(`${range.start}T00:00:00Z`);
  const end = Date.parse(`${range.end}T00:00:00Z`);
  if (!Number.isFinite(start) || !Number.isFinite(end) || start > end) return true;
  const first = start - DAY;
  const last = end + 2 * DAY;
```

with:

```ts
}

function spanDays(range: { start: string; end: string }): number {
  return (Date.parse(`${range.end}T00:00:00Z`) - Date.parse(`${range.start}T00:00:00Z`)) / DAY + 1;
}

// Whether two zones have different UTC offsets at any instant that can fall on
// a calendar day of `range` in either of them. Offsets are compared at every
// UTC midnight from the day before `start` to two days after `end`; a day on
// which either zone changes offset is also checked every 15 minutes. A range
// that cannot be read, that ends before it starts, or that is longer than
// MAX_COMPARED_DAYS proves nothing, so it counts as differing. Answers are
// remembered.
export function offsetsDiffer(a: string, b: string, range: { start: string; end: string }): boolean {
  const key = `${a}|${b}|${range.start}|${range.end}`;
  let differ = compared.get(key);
  if (differ === undefined) {
    differ = compareOffsets(a, b, range);
    compared.set(key, differ);
  }
  return differ;
}

function compareOffsets(a: string, b: string, range: { start: string; end: string }): boolean {
  const start = Date.parse(`${range.start}T00:00:00Z`);
  const end = Date.parse(`${range.end}T00:00:00Z`);
  if (!Number.isFinite(start) || !Number.isFinite(end) || start > end) return true;
  if (spanDays(range) > MAX_COMPARED_DAYS) return true;
  const first = start - DAY;
  const last = end + 2 * DAY;
```

```ts
// The zone GAM counted a report's days in, from its definition's time zone
// source (spec §2.4), or null when this server cannot name it: a report
// created elsewhere may use the Ad Exchange zone, or not say.
export function reportZoneOf(definition: { timeZoneSource?: string; timeZone?: string }, networkZone: string): string | null {
  switch (definition.timeZoneSource) {
    case 'PUBLISHER':
      return networkZone;
    case 'UTC':
      return 'UTC';
    case 'PROVIDED':
      return definition.timeZone ? canonicalTimeZone(definition.timeZone) : null;
    default:
      return null;
  }
}

// The warning for a result whose report zone this server cannot name.
export function unknownZoneWarning(dateZone: string, source: string | undefined): string {
  return `GAM counted this report's days in a time zone this server cannot name (time zone source ${source ?? 'not stated'}), ` +
    `so they may not cover the same hours as dates chosen in ${dateZone} (GAM_TIMEZONE).`;
}
```

### Task 12: The HTTP endpoint checks Host, a bearer token and body size (spec §2.7, G1–G5)

`connectHttp` takes `bearerToken?` and `allowedHosts?` and checks, before the SDK sees a request: `/healthz` first (always 200, only `{"status":"ok"}`, no token and no Host check, so probes that address the container directly work); then the Host (403 `Host not allowed` unless its host name, port ignored, is listed); then the token (401 `Unauthorized` with `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer`); then the path; then the body. Refusals are JSON-RPC error bodies, as the SDK writes its own.

- **Token.** `Authorization: Bearer <token>`, scheme case-insensitive, nothing after the token. Both the presented and the configured token are hashed with SHA-256 and the digests compared with `crypto.timingSafeEqual`, so tokens of different lengths are compared without throwing and without a length-dependent shortcut. The header is never logged; `http.refused` logs only `no bearer token` or `wrong bearer token`.
- **Unauthenticated.** Without a token, one `http.unauthenticated` warning is logged when the server starts listening.
- **Body.** Over `MAX_BODY_BYTES` (1 MiB): a declared `Content-Length` over the limit is refused before reading, so Node discards the body and the connection stays usable; a body without a length is read with event listeners (not `for await`, whose early exit destroys the socket before a reply can be written) and refused where it crosses the limit, with `Connection: close`. `_internal.readJsonBody` keeps its signature, so `tests/http-transport.test.ts` is unchanged.
- **Host.** SDK 1.30 deprecates the transport's `allowedHosts` / `enableDnsRebindingProtection` in favour of middleware, and they compare the whole Host header including the port (see SDK facts). The SDK's replacement middleware is written for Express. `connectHttp` therefore checks the host name itself, parsed as the middleware parses it (`hostnameOf`, new `src/parse.ts`), and never sets the deprecated options. Spec §2.7 is reworded in this task to say so.
- **Listening.** A failed `listen` rejects with `HttpListenError`, whose message names `GAM_HTTP_PORT` (Task 13 turns it into exit code 2).

The new tests use raw `node:http` requests so they can set the Host header and choose the body framing, and a recording logger to check that neither the configured token nor a wrong one reaches the log.

**Files:**
- Create: `src/parse.ts`, `tests/http-boundary.test.ts`, `tests/parse.test.ts`
- Modify: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-17-gam-mcp-0.6.0-design.md`, `src/http-transport.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `createLogger`, the SDK `Server`.
- Produces:
  - `src/http-transport.ts`: `MAX_BODY_BYTES = 1024 * 1024`; `type HttpOptions = { port: number; logger: Logger; bearerToken?: string; allowedHosts?: string[] }`; `connectHttp(createMcpServer: () => Server, opts: HttpOptions): Promise<{ port: number; close: () => Promise<void> }>`; `class HttpListenError extends Error { port: number; code: string }`.
  - `src/parse.ts`: `hostnameOf(host: string): string | null`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

Create `tests/http-boundary.test.ts`:

```ts
import { describe, it, expect } from 'vitest';
import { request } from 'node:http';
import { Server } from '@modelcontextprotocol/sdk/server/index.js';
import { connectHttp, HttpListenError, MAX_BODY_BYTES, type HttpOptions } from '../src/http-transport.js';
import { createLogger } from '../src/log.js';

const TOKEN = 'test-token-7Qx9-not-a-real-secret';
const WRONG = 'wrong-token-3Kd1';

const initialize = JSON.stringify({
  jsonrpc: '2.0',
  id: 1,
  method: 'initialize',
  params: { protocolVersion: '2025-03-26', capabilities: {}, clientInfo: { name: 'boundary-test', version: '1' } },
});

type Reply = { status: number; headers: Record<string, string | string[] | undefined>; body: string };

// A raw request, so the Host header and the body framing are the test's to choose.
function send(
  port: number,
  opts: { method?: string; path?: string; headers?: Record<string, string>; body?: string | Buffer; chunked?: boolean },
): Promise<Reply> {
  return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
    const body = opts.body === undefined ? undefined : Buffer.from(opts.body);
    const req = request({
      host: '127.0.0.1',
      port,
      method: opts.method ?? 'POST',
      path: opts.path ?? '/mcp',
      headers: {
        'content-type': 'application/json',
        accept: 'application/json, text/event-stream',
        ...(body && !opts.chunked && { 'content-length': String(body.length) }),
        ...opts.headers,
      },
    }, (res) => {
      const chunks: Buffer[] = [];
      res.on('data', (c: Buffer) => chunks.push(c));
      res.on('end', () => resolve({ status: res.statusCode ?? 0, headers: res.headers, body: Buffer.concat(chunks).toString('utf8') }));
    });
    // The server may answer and close before a refused body is fully sent.
    req.on('error', (e: NodeJS.ErrnoException) => (e.code === 'EPIPE' || e.code === 'ECONNRESET' ? undefined : reject(e)));
    if (body && opts.chunked) {
      // Written in pieces without a length, so the server only learns the size by reading.
      for (let i = 0; i < body.length; i += 64 * 1024) req.write(body.subarray(i, i + 64 * 1024));
    } else if (body) {
      req.write(body);
    }
    req.end();
  });
}

function recorder() {
  const lines: string[] = [];
  const logger = createLogger({ level: 'debug', sink: (l) => { lines.push(l); } });
  return { logger, lines, events: () => lines.map((l) => JSON.parse(l) as Record<string, unknown>) };
}

async function withServer(options: Omit<HttpOptions, 'port' | 'logger'>, run: (port: number, log: ReturnType<typeof recorder>) => Promise<void>) {
  const log = recorder();
  const handle = await connectHttp(
    () => new Server({ name: 'test', version: '1' }, { capabilities: { tools: {} } }),
    { port: 0, logger: log.logger, ...options },
  );
  try {
    await run(handle.port, log);
  } finally {
    await handle.close();
  }
}

describe('bearer token (spec §2.7)', () => {
  it('G1: with a token set, a request without it or with another is 401; with it, 200', async () => {
    await withServer({ bearerToken: TOKEN }, async (port, log) => {
      const missing = await send(port, { body: initialize });
      expect(missing.status).toBe(401);
      expect(missing.headers['www-authenticate']).toBe('Bearer');
      expect(JSON.parse(missing.body)).toEqual({ jsonrpc: '2.0', error: { code: -32000, message: 'Unauthorized' }, id: null });

      for (const authorization of [`Bearer ${WRONG}`, `Bearer ${TOKEN}x`, `Basic ${TOKEN}`, `Bearer ${TOKEN} extra`, 'Bearer', '']) {
        expect((await send(port, { body: initialize, headers: { authorization } })).status, authorization).toBe(401);
      }
      expect((await send(port, { path: '/elsewhere', body: '{}' })).status).toBe(401);

      expect((await send(port, { body: initialize, headers: { authorization: `Bearer ${TOKEN}` } })).status).toBe(200);
      expect((await send(port, { body: initialize, headers: { authorization: `bearer  ${TOKEN}` } })).status).toBe(200);

      expect(log.events().filter((e) => e.msg === 'http.refused').map((e) => e.reason)).toContain('no bearer token');
      expect(log.lines.join('')).not.toContain(TOKEN);
      expect(log.lines.join('')).not.toContain(WRONG);
    });
  });

  it('G2: /healthz answers without a token and says only that the server is up', async () => {
    await withServer({ bearerToken: TOKEN, allowedHosts: ['gam-mcp.internal'] }, async (port) => {
      const health = await send(port, { method: 'GET', path: '/healthz' });
      expect(health.status).toBe(200);
      expect(health.body).toBe('{"status":"ok"}');
    });
  });

  it('G3: without a token the server warns once, at startup, and serves', async () => {
    await withServer({}, async (port, log) => {
      for (let i = 0; i < 3; i++) expect((await send(port, { body: initialize })).status).toBe(200);
      expect(log.events().filter((e) => e.level === 'warn').map((e) => e.msg)).toEqual(['http.unauthenticated']);
    });
    await withServer({ bearerToken: TOKEN }, async (_port, log) => {
      expect(log.events().map((e) => e.msg)).not.toContain('http.unauthenticated');
    });
  });
});

describe('request size (spec §2.7)', () => {
  const big = JSON.stringify({ padding: 'x'.repeat(1.1 * 1024 * 1024) });

  it('G4: a 1.1 MB body is 413, and the next request on the server succeeds', async () => {
    await withServer({ bearerToken: TOKEN }, async (port) => {
      const auth = { authorization: `Bearer ${TOKEN}` };
      const refused = await send(port, { body: big, headers: auth });
      expect(refused.status).toBe(413);
      expect(JSON.parse(refused.body).error.message).toBe(`Request body is over ${MAX_BODY_BYTES} bytes`);
      expect((await send(port, { body: initialize, headers: auth })).status).toBe(200);
    });
  });

  it('G4: a body sent without a length is refused where it passes the limit', async () => {
    await withServer({}, async (port) => {
      const refused = await send(port, { body: big, chunked: true });
      expect(refused.status).toBe(413);
      expect(refused.headers.connection).toBe('close');
      expect((await send(port, { body: initialize })).status).toBe(200);
    });
  });

  it('a body of exactly the limit is read', async () => {
    await withServer({}, async (port) => {
      const exact = initialize.padEnd(MAX_BODY_BYTES, ' ');
      expect((await send(port, { body: exact })).status).toBe(200);
    });
  });
});

describe('allowed hosts (spec §2.7)', () => {
  it('G5: with hosts set, a request naming another host is 403, whatever the port', async () => {
    await withServer({ allowedHosts: ['gam-mcp.internal', '127.0.0.1'] }, async (port, log) => {
      const foreign = await send(port, { body: initialize, headers: { host: 'attacker.example' } });
      expect(foreign.status).toBe(403);
      expect(JSON.parse(foreign.body).error.message).toBe('Host not allowed');
      expect((await send(port, { body: initialize, headers: { host: 'gam-mcp.internal.attacker.example' } })).status).toBe(403);
      expect((await send(port, { body: initialize, headers: { host: 'bad host' } })).status).toBe(403);
      expect((await send(port, { body: initialize, headers: { host: 'GAM-MCP.internal:8080' } })).status).toBe(200);
      expect((await send(port, { body: initialize })).status).toBe(200); // Host: 127.0.0.1:<port>
      expect(log.events().find((e) => e.msg === 'http.refused')).toMatchObject({ status: 403, host: 'attacker.example' });
    });
  });

  it('G5: the host is checked before the token', async () => {
    await withServer({ allowedHosts: ['gam-mcp.internal'], bearerToken: TOKEN }, async (port) => {
      const r = await send(port, { body: initialize, headers: { host: 'attacker.example', authorization: `Bearer ${TOKEN}` } });
      expect(r.status).toBe(403);
    });
  });

  it('G5: without hosts set, any Host is served', async () => {
    await withServer({}, async (port) => {
      expect((await send(port, { body: initialize, headers: { host: 'attacker.example' } })).status).toBe(200);
    });
  });
});

describe('listening (PR 2 review)', () => {
  it('a port already in use is an HttpListenError naming GAM_HTTP_PORT', async () => {
    await withServer({}, async (port) => {
      const log = recorder();
      const second = connectHttp(() => new Server({ name: 'test', version: '1' }, { capabilities: {} }), { port, logger: log.logger });
      await expect(second).rejects.toThrow(HttpListenError);
      await expect(second).rejects.toThrow(`GAM_HTTP_PORT ${port} is already in use by another process`);
      expect(log.events().map((e) => e.msg)).not.toContain('http.listening');
    });
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Guard the endpoint**

Create `src/parse.ts`:

```ts
// Parsers for values that arrive as text: settings and request headers.

// The lower-case host name a Host header (or a host list entry) names,
// without its port, or null. Parsed as the SDK's hostHeaderValidation
// middleware parses it.
export function hostnameOf(host: string): string | null {
  try {
    return new URL(`http://${host}`).hostname;
  } catch {
    return null;
  }
}
```

Replace the whole of `src/http-transport.ts` with:

```ts
// HTTP/SSE transport for hosted deployment. Opt-in via GAM_HTTP_PORT env var;
// when unset (default), the MCP runs over stdio exactly as before.
//
// Uses the SDK's StreamableHTTPServerTransport in stateless mode (no session
// IDs) — each request is logically independent and there's no in-flight
// state worth persisting across requests.
//
// Stateless means a NEW server and transport per request. A single transport
// hoisted out of the request handler is spent once its first request finishes:
// every later request then fails, and the SDK writes that 500 itself without
// throwing, so nothing reaches the catch below and nothing is logged. That was
// the shape of the bug fixed in 0.5.2 — see the regression test, which asserts
// several sequential requests rather than one.
//
// The boundary (spec §2.7) is checked here, before a request reaches the SDK:
// the Host header, then the bearer token, then the body size. The SDK 1.30
// transport's own allowedHosts / enableDnsRebindingProtection options are
// deprecated in favour of middleware and compare the whole Host header, port
// included, so this file checks the host name the way the SDK's
// hostHeaderValidation middleware does.

import { createHash, timingSafeEqual } from 'node:crypto';
import { createServer, type IncomingMessage, type ServerResponse } from 'node:http';
import { StreamableHTTPServerTransport } from '@modelcontextprotocol/sdk/server/streamableHttp.js';
import type { Server } from '@modelcontextprotocol/sdk/server/index.js';
import type { Logger } from './log.js';
import { hostnameOf } from './parse.js';

// The largest request body served; a larger one is answered 413.
export const MAX_BODY_BYTES = 1024 * 1024;

export type HttpOptions = {
  port: number;
  logger: Logger;
  // When set, every request but /healthz must carry `Authorization: Bearer <token>`.
  bearerToken?: string;
  // When set, a request whose Host header names another host is refused.
  // Lower-case host names without ports.
  allowedHosts?: string[];
};

// The port could not be listened on. The message names GAM_HTTP_PORT.
export class HttpListenError extends Error {
  constructor(readonly port: number, readonly code: string) {
    super(
      code === 'EADDRINUSE'
        ? `GAM_HTTP_PORT ${port} is already in use by another process`
        : `GAM_HTTP_PORT ${port} cannot be listened on (${code})`,
    );
    this.name = 'HttpListenError';
  }
}

class BodyTooLargeError extends Error {
  constructor(readonly whileReading: boolean) {
    super(`request body is over ${MAX_BODY_BYTES} bytes`);
    this.name = 'BodyTooLargeError';
  }
}

// Whether an Authorization header carries this token. Both sides are hashed
// first, so tokens of different lengths are compared without throwing and in
// the same time as equal-length ones.
function tokenCheck(expected: string): (header: string | undefined) => boolean {
  const want = createHash('sha256').update(expected).digest();
  return (header) => {
    const [scheme, token, ...rest] = (header ?? '').split(' ').filter((part) => part !== '');
    if (scheme?.toLowerCase() !== 'bearer' || token === undefined || rest.length > 0) return false;
    return timingSafeEqual(createHash('sha256').update(token).digest(), want);
  };
}

// A JSON-RPC error body, as the SDK writes its own refusals.
function refuse(res: ServerResponse, status: number, message: string, headers: Record<string, string> = {}): void {
  res.writeHead(status, { 'content-type': 'application/json', ...headers });
  res.end(JSON.stringify({ jsonrpc: '2.0', error: { code: -32000, message }, id: null }));
}

export async function connectHttp(
  // A factory, not a server: one instance cannot be reused across requests.
  createMcpServer: () => Server,
  opts: HttpOptions,
): Promise<{ port: number; close: () => Promise<void> }> {
  const authorized = opts.bearerToken === undefined ? null : tokenCheck(opts.bearerToken);
  const httpServer = createServer(async (req, res) => {
    // Health endpoint without authentication or a Host check, so probes that
    // address the container directly work. It says nothing but "up".
    if (req.url === '/healthz') {
      res.statusCode = 200;
      res.setHeader('content-type', 'application/json');
      res.end(JSON.stringify({ status: 'ok' }));
      return;
    }
    if (opts.allowedHosts) {
      const host = req.headers.host;
      const name = host === undefined ? null : hostnameOf(host);
      if (name === null || !opts.allowedHosts.includes(name)) {
        opts.logger.warn('http.refused', { status: 403, reason: 'host not allowed', host: host ?? null });
        refuse(res, 403, 'Host not allowed');
        return;
      }
    }
    if (authorized && !authorized(req.headers.authorization)) {
      // Never log the header: it may hold a real token for something else.
      const reason = req.headers.authorization === undefined ? 'no bearer token' : 'wrong bearer token';
      opts.logger.warn('http.refused', { status: 401, reason });
      refuse(res, 401, 'Unauthorized', { 'www-authenticate': 'Bearer' });
      return;
    }
    if (req.url !== '/mcp' && req.url !== '/mcp/') {
      res.statusCode = 404;
      res.end('not found');
      return;
    }
    let server: Server | undefined;
    let transport: StreamableHTTPServerTransport | undefined;
    try {
      const body = await readJsonBody(req);
      server = createMcpServer();
      transport = new StreamableHTTPServerTransport({
        sessionIdGenerator: undefined, // stateless
      });
      await server.connect(transport);
      await transport.handleRequest(req, res, body);
    } catch (e) {
      if (e instanceof BodyTooLargeError) {
        opts.logger.warn('http.refused', { status: 413, reason: e.message });
        // A body cut off part-way cannot be drained, so that connection closes.
        refuse(res, 413, `Request body is over ${MAX_BODY_BYTES} bytes`, e.whileReading ? { connection: 'close' } : {});
        return;
      }
      opts.logger.error('http.handle_error', { message: e instanceof Error ? e.message : String(e) });
      if (!res.headersSent) {
        res.statusCode = 500;
        res.setHeader('content-type', 'application/json');
        res.end(JSON.stringify({ error: 'internal_error' }));
      }
    } finally {
      // Release this request's pair once the response is done. Closing earlier
      // would cut off a streaming (SSE) response mid-flight.
      const release = () => {
        void transport?.close().catch(() => {});
        void server?.close().catch(() => {});
      };
      if (res.writableEnded) release();
      else res.once('close', release);
    }
  });

  await new Promise<void>((resolve, reject) => {
    httpServer.once('error', (e: NodeJS.ErrnoException) => reject(new HttpListenError(opts.port, e.code ?? 'unknown error')));
    httpServer.listen(opts.port, () => {
      opts.logger.info('http.listening', { port: opts.port, endpoint: '/mcp' });
      resolve();
    });
  });
  if (authorized === null) {
    opts.logger.warn('http.unauthenticated', {
      reason: 'GAM_HTTP_BEARER_TOKEN is not set, so every request to /mcp is served without authentication',
    });
  }

  const shutdown = () => {
    opts.logger.info('http.shutdown');
    httpServer.close(() => process.exit(0));
    // Hard exit if shutdown hangs (held-open SSE streams etc).
    setTimeout(() => process.exit(0), 2000).unref();
  };
  process.on('SIGINT', shutdown);
  process.on('SIGTERM', shutdown);

  const address = httpServer.address();
  const boundPort = typeof address === 'object' && address ? address.port : opts.port;

  return {
    // The port actually bound, which differs from opts.port when 0 is passed.
    port: boundPort,
    close: () =>
      new Promise<void>((resolve) => {
        process.off('SIGINT', shutdown);
        process.off('SIGTERM', shutdown);
        httpServer.close(() => resolve());
      }),
  };
}

// Reads and parses a request body of at most MAX_BODY_BYTES. A declared
// length over the limit is refused before reading, so Node discards the body
// and the connection stays usable; a body that grows past the limit is
// refused where it crosses it.
function readJsonBody(req: IncomingMessage): Promise<unknown> {
  // GET requests have no body (SSE streams open on GET).
  if (req.method === 'GET' || req.method === 'HEAD') return Promise.resolve(undefined);
  if (Number(req.headers['content-length'] ?? 0) > MAX_BODY_BYTES) {
    return Promise.reject(new BodyTooLargeError(false));
  }
  return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
    const chunks: Buffer[] = [];
    let size = 0;
    const onData = (chunk: Buffer) => {
      size += chunk.length;
      if (size > MAX_BODY_BYTES) {
        req.off('data', onData);
        req.off('end', onEnd);
        req.pause();
        reject(new BodyTooLargeError(true));
        return;
      }
      chunks.push(chunk);
    };
    const onEnd = () => {
      const text = Buffer.concat(chunks).toString('utf8');
      if (!text.trim()) {
        resolve(undefined);
        return;
      }
      try {
        resolve(JSON.parse(text));
      } catch {
        reject(new Error('request body is not valid JSON'));
      }
    };
    req.on('data', onData);
    req.once('end', onEnd);
    req.once('error', reject);
  });
}

// Provided for unit testing — never used by the HTTP listener itself.
// Kept here so the body-reading edge cases are testable without spinning
// up a real server.
export const _internal = { readJsonBody };

// Make ServerResponse type accessible for handlers without an extra import.
export type { ServerResponse };
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Reword spec §2.7 to match the SDK**

In `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-17-gam-mcp-0.6.0-design.md`:

Replace:

```markdown
- `GAM_HTTP_BEARER_TOKEN`: when set, every request except `/healthz` must carry `Authorization: Bearer <token>`, compared with `crypto.timingSafeEqual`; otherwise `401`. When unset, startup logs a warning once that the endpoint is unauthenticated.
- Request bodies over 1 MB get `413`; the server keeps serving.
- `GAM_HTTP_ALLOWED_HOSTS` (comma-separated): when set, the SDK's DNS-rebinding protection is enabled with that list, and other `Host` headers get `403`. When unset, no Host check.
- `/healthz` stays unauthenticated and returns only `{"status":"ok"}`. Because startup now checks the credential (§2.4), a running process has a working one.

```

with:

```markdown
- `GAM_HTTP_BEARER_TOKEN`: when set, every request except `/healthz` must carry `Authorization: Bearer <token>`, compared with `crypto.timingSafeEqual`; otherwise `401`. When unset, startup logs a warning once that the endpoint is unauthenticated.
- Request bodies over 1 MiB (1,048,576 bytes) get `413`; the server keeps serving.
- `GAM_HTTP_ALLOWED_HOSTS` (comma-separated host names, without ports): when set, a request whose `Host` header names another host gets `403`, checked before the token. The host name is compared without its port, as the SDK's `hostHeaderValidation` middleware compares it. The transport's own `allowedHosts` and `enableDnsRebindingProtection` options are deprecated in SDK 1.30 in favour of that middleware, which is written for Express, and they compare the whole header, port included, so the server does the check itself. When unset, no Host check.
- `/healthz` stays unauthenticated, skips the Host check, and returns only `{"status":"ok"}`. Because startup now checks the credential (§2.4), a running process has a working one.
- An invalid `GAM_HTTP_PORT`, `GAM_HTTP_BEARER_TOKEN` (anything but printable ASCII without spaces) or `GAM_HTTP_ALLOWED_HOSTS`, or a port another process holds, stops startup with exit code 2, naming the variable and never quoting the token.

```

---

### Task 13: HTTP settings are read as strictly as the rest, and a busy port stops startup (PR 2 follow-ups)

`readHttpSettings(env)` reads `GAM_HTTP_PORT`, `GAM_HTTP_BEARER_TOKEN` and `GAM_HTTP_ALLOWED_HOSTS` (configuration table above) and returns `null` in stdio mode. `wholeNumber` (in `src/parse.ts`) is the one digits-only reader, now used for `GAM_HTTP_PORT`, `GAM_PAGE_ROWS`, `GAM_REPORT_TIMEOUT_MS` and `GAM_REPORT_CACHE_TTL_MS` (PR 2 follow-up): `1e3`, ` 5000`, `0x10` and `60000.0` are refused, and the `GAM_PAGE_ROWS` error now quotes its value, as the port error already did. The token must be printable ASCII without spaces (anything else could never match a header) and its error never quotes it.

`src/cli.ts` reads the HTTP settings first (so a bad one stops startup before the credential is used), logs `config.ignored` for an HTTP-only setting in stdio mode, passes the token and hosts to `connectHttp`, and turns `HttpListenError` into exit code 2 (PR 2 follow-up: a port already in use). That last mapping is not covered by a real-process test: reaching `listen` requires a credential that passes the startup check, and the API base URL is fixed, so a hermetic test would need a test-only hook in production code. `tests/http-boundary.test.ts` covers the error itself; the mapping is four lines in `main()`.

The real-process test in `tests/cli-startup.test.ts` builds `dist/` in its `beforeAll`, as before.

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/cli.ts`, `src/parse.ts`, `src/report-exec.ts`, `src/startup.ts`, `tests/cli-startup.test.ts`, `tests/parse.test.ts`, `tests/report-exec.test.ts`, `tests/startup.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `hostnameOf` (Task 12), `HttpListenError`, `connectHttp`, `connectStdio`, `buildMcpServer`.
- Produces:
  - `src/parse.ts`: `wholeNumber(raw: string): number | null`.
  - `src/startup.ts`: `type HttpSettings = { port: number; bearerToken: string | undefined; allowedHosts: string[] | undefined }`; `readHttpSettings(env: NodeJS.ProcessEnv): HttpSettings | null | { error: string }`; `ignoredInStdio(env: NodeJS.ProcessEnv): string[]`; the integer settings use `wholeNumber`.
  - `src/report-exec.ts`: `resolvePageRows` uses `wholeNumber` and quotes the value in its error.

```ts
describe('readHttpSettings (spec §2.7)', () => {
  const PORT = { GAM_HTTP_PORT: '8080' };

  it('is null in stdio mode', () => {
    expect(readHttpSettings({})).toBeNull();
    expect(readHttpSettings({ GAM_HTTP_PORT: '', GAM_HTTP_BEARER_TOKEN: 'x' })).toBeNull();
  });

  it('reads the port, the token and the host list', () => {
    expect(readHttpSettings(PORT)).toEqual({ port: 8080, bearerToken: undefined, allowedHosts: undefined });
    expect(readHttpSettings({
      ...PORT,
      GAM_HTTP_BEARER_TOKEN: 'abc.DEF-123_~+/=',
      GAM_HTTP_ALLOWED_HOSTS: ' GAM-MCP.internal, localhost ,,[::1]',
    })).toEqual({ port: 8080, bearerToken: 'abc.DEF-123_~+/=', allowedHosts: ['gam-mcp.internal', 'localhost', '[::1]'] });
  });

  it('treats empty token and host values as unset', () => {
    expect(readHttpSettings({ ...PORT, GAM_HTTP_BEARER_TOKEN: '', GAM_HTTP_ALLOWED_HOSTS: '  ' }))
      .toEqual({ port: 8080, bearerToken: undefined, allowedHosts: undefined });
  });

  it.each(['0', '65536', '80.5', '1e3', ' 80', 'http'])('refuses GAM_HTTP_PORT=%j, quoting it', (port) => {
    expect(readHttpSettings({ GAM_HTTP_PORT: port })).toEqual({
      error: `GAM_HTTP_PORT must be a valid port number, got ${JSON.stringify(port)}`,
    });
  });

  // A tab and an accented letter, built from their codes.
  const TAB = String.fromCharCode(9);
  const E_ACUTE = String.fromCharCode(233);
  it.each(['has space', `tab${TAB}here`, `caf${E_ACUTE}`])('refuses a token that is not printable ASCII, without quoting it', (token) => {
    const r = readHttpSettings({ ...PORT, GAM_HTTP_BEARER_TOKEN: token });
    expect(r).toEqual({ error: 'GAM_HTTP_BEARER_TOKEN must be printable ASCII with no spaces' });
  });

  it.each([
    ['gam-mcp.internal:8080', 'gam-mcp.internal:8080'],
    ['http://gam-mcp.internal', 'http://gam-mcp.internal'],
    ['gam-mcp.internal/mcp', 'gam-mcp.internal/mcp'],
    ['a.internal,bad host', 'bad host'],
  ])('refuses GAM_HTTP_ALLOWED_HOSTS=%j, naming the entry', (hosts, entry) => {
    expect(readHttpSettings({ ...PORT, GAM_HTTP_ALLOWED_HOSTS: hosts })).toEqual({
      error: `GAM_HTTP_ALLOWED_HOSTS must list host names without ports, separated by commas; ${JSON.stringify(entry)} is not one`,
    });
  });

  it('refuses a host list with no host in it', () => {
    expect(readHttpSettings({ ...PORT, GAM_HTTP_ALLOWED_HOSTS: ' , ' })).toEqual({
      error: 'GAM_HTTP_ALLOWED_HOSTS names no host: " , "',
    });
  });

  it('names the HTTP settings a stdio process ignores', () => {
    expect(ignoredInStdio({ GAM_HTTP_BEARER_TOKEN: 'x', GAM_HTTP_ALLOWED_HOSTS: 'h' }))
      .toEqual(['GAM_HTTP_BEARER_TOKEN', 'GAM_HTTP_ALLOWED_HOSTS']);
    expect(ignoredInStdio({ GAM_HTTP_BEARER_TOKEN: '' })).toEqual([]);
  });
```

```ts
// Parsers for values that arrive as text: settings and request headers.

// A whole number written with digits only, or null. Every integer setting is
// read with this: Number() alone would accept "1e3", " 5", "0x10" and "5.0".
export function wholeNumber(raw: string): number | null {
  if (!/^\d+$/.test(raw)) return null;
  const n = Number(raw);
  return Number.isSafeInteger(n) ? n : null;
}

```
